Transmission price controls and BETTA



Final proposals and impact assessment

December 2004     279/04
Summary

Currently, the National Grid Company plc (NGC) owns and operates the electricity
transmission system in England & Wales, and SP Transmission Ltd (SP Transmission) and
Scottish Hydro-Electric Transmission Ltd (SHETL) own and operate their transmission
systems in the south and north of Scotland respectively. All these systems are
connected, with an interconnector connecting the transmission systems of SP
Transmission and NGC (the England-Scotland interconnector).

Following Royal Assent on 22 July 2004, the Energy Act 2004 provides the Secretary of
State with powers to introduce the British Electricity Trading and Transmission
Arrangements (BETTA). Under BETTA the assets comprising the England-Scotland
interconnector will be incorporated into the respective transmission systems of SP
Transmission and NGC, and NGC will be system operator for all three transmission
systems in GB (the GB transmission system), with existing licensees continuing to own
their transmission system assets. The planned go-live date for BETTA is 1 April 2005.

The current transmission price controls for SHETL and SP Transmission are intended to
last until 31 March 2005. It is proposed to roll forward these price controls for two years
to 31 March 2007 to align the price control review dates with those for other
transmission licensees in both electricity and gas, enabling all transmission issues to be
considered together at the next review. In addition, from BETTA go-live, the price
controls of all three transmission licensees will need to be adjusted to provide
remuneration according to the licensees’ changed roles and responsibilities under
BETTA.

This document sets out Ofgem’s final proposals for:

♦        the roll forward price controls that would apply to SP Transmission and SHETL if
         BETTA go-live were delayed beyond 1 April 2005, and

♦        for the price controls to apply to SP Transmission, SHETL and NGC under
         BETTA1.




1
 This document does not address NGC’s SO external cost incentives to apply on a GB basis from1 April
2005. These incentives are being taken forward through a separate consultation process.
In setting these final proposals, Ofgem has determined two issues that significantly affect
the level of allowed revenues:

♦      the cost of capital assumptions for SP Transmission and SHETL. It is proposed to
       align these with those proposed as part of the full post-tax approach developed as
       part of the recent distribution price control review (based on a 4.8% post-tax cost
       of capital). Since July further consideration has been given to determining the tax
       allowance appropriate to transmission. Given the limited nature of the roll
       forward review, the approach in these proposals is to increase the pre-tax cost of
       capital for SP Transmission and SHETL to allow for the level of tax allowances
       applicable to SP Transmission and SHETL’s respective affiliated distribution
       companies, and

♦      the value at which it is appropriate to include the England-Scotland
       interconnector assets into the Regulatory Asset Values (RAVs) of the respective
       transmission licensees under BETTA. Ofgem has determined values consistent
       with contract revenues yielding a 10% rate of return on the relevant assets in the
       absence of BETTA.

The overall impact of the proposals is an increase in transmission revenues in 2005/6 of
5% (GB basis). However, this includes the impact of some measures which represent a
reallocation of costs between connectees and users of the GB transmission system, and
between interconnector users and users of the transmission system. Excluding these
measures, the net impact on customers is an increase in transmission charges of 2.5%
(GB basis). Overall, it is considered that the impact of the proposals on customers is
low.

The impacts of the final proposals in this paper are discussed in more detail in the Impact
Assessment in Appendix 3 to this paper.

This document also presents draft licence modifications to implement the price control
proposals to apply under BETTA.

   1. Introduction
1.1.    This document sets out:

         ♦        Ofgem’s final proposals for the roll forward price controls that would
                  apply to SP Transmission and SHETL if BETTA go-live were delayed
                  beyond 1 April 2005

         ♦        Ofgem’s final proposals for the price controls to apply to SP
                  Transmission, SHETL and NGC under BETTA. An important part of
                  setting these price controls is the determination of the value at which it is
                  appropriate to include the England-Scotland interconnector assets into
                  the Regulatory Asset Values (RAVs) of the respective transmission
                  licensees

         ♦        the draft licence modifications to implement the price controls to apply
                  under BETTA (and consequential amendments to the STC), and

         ♦        Ofgem’s Impact Assessment.

1.2.    Ofgem is carrying out a number of separate consultations which may affect the
        allowed revenues of licensees that are set out in this document for 2005/6 and
        2006/7. These consultations are:

         ♦        the funding of additional investment in the transmission systems of the
                  three transmission licensees to accommodate renewable generation
                  (TIRG)

         ♦        the electricity transmission network reliability incentive scheme to apply
                  to NGC, and work to introduce similar incentive arrangements for SHETL
                  and SP Transmission

         ♦        the extension of NGC’s TO price control and SO internal cost incentive
                  scheme to 2006/7, and

         ♦        NGC’s SO external cost incentives to apply on a GB basis from 1 April
                  2005.

1.3.    These consultations are summarised in Appendix 1. Any conclusions regarding
        allowed revenues resulting from these consultations will be implemented by
        making separate adjustments to the allowed revenues proposed in this
        document.


         Background

1.4.     The current Scottish transmission price controls are intended to last until 31
         March 2005. In June 2003, Ofgem consulted on extending the Scottish
         transmission price controls2, and in November 2003, Ofgem issued
         announcements on the alignment of the transmission price controls3. These
         proposed aligning all the electricity and gas transmission price controls, through
         two year roll forwards of the Scottish transmission price controls and a one year
         roll forward of NGC's electricity transmission price control.

1.5.     Ofgem’s March 20044 paper set out the proposed work programme to develop
         the transmission price controls for SP Transmission and SHETL for the years
         2005/6 and 2006/7. Ofgem’s March report noted that in a full price control
         review all the determinants of a company’s allowed revenues are examined
         thoroughly in setting the allowed revenues, but proposed a high level review
         where a price control is being rolled forwards. The reasons for this included
         proportionality, that is matching the work load of both companies and Ofgem to
         the benefits, and that carrying out a full review for a shorter period than usual
         (namely 2 years instead of 5 years) could tend to increase the perceptions of
         uncertainty for providers of finance.

1.6.     From BETTA go-live (planned for 1 April 2005), NGC will be system operator for
        the transmission system across the whole of Great Britain (GB), while SP
        Transmission and SHETL will be transmission owners with responsibility for

Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 3                                December 2004
        planning, developing and maintaining the transmission system in their respective
        areas. NGC will be responsible for agreements with users of the GB
        transmission system for connection to and use of the transmission system. To
        provide these services to users, NGC will, in addition to providing the
        transmission system in England and Wales, procure transmission services from
        SP Transmission and SHETL. This means that a number of interactions between
        transmission licensees will be required. These interactions include, amongst
        other things, arrangements for:

         ♦        the provision of transmission services by transmission owners to NGC

         ♦        the operation, including the configuration, of the GB transmission system

         ♦        the co-ordination of the planning of licensees' transmission systems

         ♦        the progression of matters necessary to respond to applications for new
                  connections (or modifications of existing connections)

         ♦        planning for, and co-ordination of, transmission outages

         ♦        the resolution of disputes, and

         ♦        the exchange of information between transmission licensees.

1.7.     The System Operator –Transmission Owner Code or STC provides the principal
        contractual basis for the interactions between transmission licensees under
        BETTA.

1.8.     Under BETTA, arrangements will continue to apply which restrict certain
        revenues of transmission licensees. However, transmission owners will
        principally derive their revenues from the GB system operator, whereas the GB
        system operator will principally derive its revenues from users. This means that
        there will be two principal cashflows associated with the transmission sector as
        follows:

         ♦        from users to NGC - NGC will derive its allowable revenues from users
                  consistent with its revenue restriction licence condition and the charging




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 4                        December 2004
                  arrangements set out in its charging methodology and the user codes,
                  and

         ♦        from NGC to non-affiliated transmission owners - the transmission
                  owners will receive their allowed revenues from NGC in accordance
                  with their charging methodologies and the STC. The transmission owners
                  will also have revenue restriction licence conditions.


         Price Controls

         Objectives for the price control review

1.9.     Ofgem’s objectives for the price control review are driven by three main factors:

         ♦        the Gas and Electricity Markets Authority’s (the Authority) statutory
                  objectives and duties

         ♦        the transmission licensees’ statutory duties and licence obligations
                  (noting that under BETTA, the electricity transmission licensees will have
                  revised statutory duties and new licence obligations), and

         ♦        other influences – including the views of consumers, licensees and other
                  interested parties and guidance received from the Secretary of State on
                  social and environmental issues.

1.10.    For SHETL and SP Transmission, the current transmission price controls expire
        on 31 March 2005. NGC’s existing revenue restrictions for its TO price control
        and its SO internal cost incentives5 are intended to last until 31 March 2006.

1.11.    Ofgem’s objectives for the price control review are as follows:

         ♦        to roll forward the price controls for SP Transmission and SHETL (absent
                  BETTA) to align the price control review dates with those for NGC
                  enabling all transmission issues (gas and electricity) to be considered




5

used the context of the STC under BETTA.


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 5                                 December 2004
                  together at the next review for 2007. It is proposed to apply these roll
                  forward price controls to SP Transmission and SHETL if BETTA were
                  delayed, and

         ♦        to develop proposals to reflect the new trading and transmission
                  arrangements that will apply under BETTA. The proposals include the
                  costs of implementing BETTA incurred by transmission licensees and
                  inclusion of the England-Scotland interconnector into the price controls.
                  These proposals are developed as adjustments to the roll forward
                  controls for SP Transmission and SHETL, and to NGC’s existing price
                  control.

        Duration of price controls

1.12.   The intended duration for the price controls proposed in this document for SP
        Transmission and SHETL price controls is as follows:

         ♦        if BETTA go-live occurs on 1 April 2005 as scheduled, the roll forward
                  price controls, adjusted to reflect the changed roles of the licensees
                  under BETTA, will apply from 1 April 2005 for the two year period until
                  31 March 2007, and

         ♦        if BETTA go-live occurs after 1 April 2005, the roll forward Scottish price
                  controls (based on the existing roles of licensees), will apply from 1 April
                  2005 until BETTA go-live, at which point the adjustments to reflect the
                  changed roles of the licensees under BETTA will be applied, and the
                  adjusted controls will apply for the remainder of the two year period
                  until 31 March 2007.

1.13.    NGC’s revenue restriction has two parts. The Part 1 revenue restrictions is often
        referred to as the TO price control. The second part has two components: Parts
        2(i) and Part 2(ii). Part 2(i) is often referred to as NGC’s SO internal cost
        incentive scheme, and Part 2(ii) as NGC’s SO external cost incentive scheme.
        NGC’s TO price control and its SO internal cost incentive scheme target is
        intended to last until 31 March 2006. As discussed above, Ofgem is consulting
        separately on the SO external cost incentive scheme to apply for 2005/6 and the

         proposals set out in this document.

         Developing price control proposals

         process for developing the roll forward price control proposals to apply to SP
         Transmission and SHETL for the two year period (2005/6 and 2006/7).

         been developed to reflect the new roles and responsibilities to apply from BETTA
         go-live. The proposed areas of adjustment were set out in Ofgem’s October
         20038 and May 2004reports.

1.16.    Ofgem’s July report9 set out:

         ♦        draft price control proposals to apply for the roll-forward price period to
                  SP Transmission and SHETL from 1 April 2005 (if BETTA is delayed for
                  any reason10)

         ♦        the approach Ofgem proposed to adopt for valuing the England-Scotland
                  interconnector, and

         ♦        draft price controls proposals for SP Transmission, SHETL, and for NGC’s
                  TO and SO internal controls to apply from BETTA go-live.

         Process

1.19.    As indicated in Ofgem’s July report, if a licensee accepts Ofgem’s final price
         control proposals in respect of that licensee, then the necessary modifications to
         the revenue restriction licence conditions will be put in place using the powers
         under section 11 of the Electricity Act 1989 (as amended) to modify licence
         conditions by agreement. If a licensee indicates that they are not prepared to
         agree to Ofgem’s proposed licence modifications11 then Ofgem would expect to
         refer the matter to the Competition Commission under section 12 of the
         Electricity Act 1989.

1.20.    Ofgem’s October 2003 document set out the arrangements that would apply in
         the event that a licensee did not accept Ofgem’ final proposals.

1.21.    Where a company rejects these proposals, Ofgem would expect to proceed
         with the licence modifications for the companies who had given their agreement
         to the modifications. If the Competition Commission subsequently makes any
         rulings on industry-wide issues, consideration would need to be given as to
         whether it would be appropriate to apply the Competition Commission’s rulings
         more generally.




11
  and subject to consideration of any responses to the section 11A notice and any direction of the Secretary
of State


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 8                                    December 2004
1.22.    Since considerations by the Competition Commission may extend beyond
         BETTA go-live, it is proposed that discussions would be held with the licensee(s)
         rejecting the proposals to agree a set of licence modifications consistent with
         BETTA which could be put in place with effect from BETTA go-live, pending the
         decision of the Competition Commission. If agreement were reached, Ofgem
         would need to consider how to implement such licence modifications. This
         could be done through the normal licence modification process (which would
         include the statutory 28 days consultation period) or Ofgem could consider with
         the Secretary of State the extent to which it would be appropriate for the
         Secretary of State to exercise her powers to direct licence modifications under
         the Energy Act 2004 (the BETTA power). If no agreement could be reached, it is
         proposed that DTI/Ofgem would consult as soon as possible on a set of special
         licence conditions to apply to the relevant licensee. Following this consultation,
         these licence conditions would be introduced by the Secretary of State using the
         BETTA power. It is necessary to ensure that appropriate modifications are put in
         place pending the Competition Commission’s decision since otherwise there
         would no price controls which were consistent with the BETTA framework that
         could be applied during the Competition Commission enquiries.


         Structure of document

1.23.   Separate chapters in this paper cover the following areas:

         ♦        Chapter 2: The roll-forward price controls to apply to SP Transmission
                  and SHETL from 1 April 2005 until BETTA go-live, should BETTA be
                  deferred for any reason

         ♦        Chapter 3: The England-Scotland interconnector regulatory assets values
                  under BETTA

         ♦        Chapter 4: The adjustments to the price controls for BETTA. These will
                  apply to SP Transmission and SHETL for the years 2005/6 and 2006/7,
                  and to NGC’s TO and SO internal controls for 2005/6, and

         ♦        Chapter 5: draft licence conditions are discussed.

         Impact assessment

1.24.    Ofgem has carried out an impact assessment on the price control proposals in
        this document. This is presented in Appendix 3.


         Timetable

1.25.    This document includes Ofgem’s final proposals for:

         ♦        the roll forward price controls that would apply to SP Transmission and
                  SHETL if BETTA go-live were delayed beyond 1 April 2005, and

         ♦        the price controls to apply to SP Transmission, SHETL and NGC under
                  BETTA.

1.26.    This document also presents draft licence modifications to implement the price
        control proposals to apply under BETTA.

1.27.    Ofgem recognises that these draft licence modifications require further
        development and proposes to continue to do so in discussions with the
        transmission licensees. Two sets of licence conditions will be developed, one
        for the case where BETTA go-live is 1 April 2005 or shortly afterwards, and one
        for the case where BETTA go-live is significantly delayed.

        BETTA go-live date

1.28.    Based on information available in March 2005 on the actual BETTA go-live date
        and whether it will be different from 1 April 2005, Ofgem will decide in March
        2005 which of the sets of licence conditions developed through this document it
        is appropriate to determine for the period commencing 1 April 2005. This will
        include deciding whether it is appropriate to determine at that time the licence
        modification for BETTA for NGC.

1.29.    Ofgem recognises that if BETTA go-live is significantly delayed from 1 April
        2005 then further consultations will be required on the licence conditions to
        apply from BETTA go-live, since the proposed revenues set out in this document
        may no longer be appropriate.

1.30.    If a licensee does not accept these proposals then it will be necessary to make a
        reference to the Competition Commission, which will consider these matters and
        report in due course. Ofgem would then bring forward the necessary licence
        modifications to put into effect the recommendations of the Competition
        Commission.

1.33.    Ofgem has written to all of the transmission companies asking them to confirm,
        by 28 January 2005, whether they accept these proposals in principle.

1.34.    If companies accept these proposals, Ofgem will need to exercise its powers
        under Section 11 of the Electricity Act to propose licence modifications to which
        the companies will need to formally agree. Therefore, alongside these final
        proposals, Ofgem has published for comment draft licence modifications for the
        implementation of the BETTA price controls. Following further discussion with
        licensees, it is envisaged that the modification process will commence in early
        February 2005.

      2. Roll forward price controls for SP
           Transmission and SHETL
2.1.        This chapter sets out Ofgem’s final proposals for SP Transmission and SHETL for
            the roll forward price controls for the two year period (2005/6 and 2006/7)
            excluding any adjustments to reflect the BETTA changes. These proposals will
            form the base against which adjustments to the price controls for BETTA will be
            made and which would apply if BETTA go-live is deferred for any reason.

2.2.        Ofgem’s July report concluded that the roll forward price control should apply
            for the two year period from 1 April 2005 to 31 March 2007, and that the RPI-X
            form of control should be retained. The July report set out Ofgem’s draft price
            control proposals.

2.3.        The final proposals set out in this chapter reflect consideration of the responses
            to the July report and further discussions with the licensees. This chapter sets
            out the operating expenditure and capital expenditure allowances, the cost of
            capital
            assumptions and the allowed revenues for SP Transmission and SHETL proposed
            as part of Ofgem’s final price control proposals.


            Operating and capital expenditures in 2005/6 and
            2006/7

            forecast by SP Transmission and SHETL respectively over the current price
            control period (2000/1 to 2004/5). It also shows the projections for 2005/6 and
            2006/7 proposed in Ofgem’s draft proposals12.

2.5.     Ofgem’s July report also proposed reporting arrangements to inform the
         development of possible capital and operating expenditure incentive
         mechanisms as part of the main review for 2007 and to monitor the programme
         of proposed capital expenditure projects.

         Respondents’ Views

2.6.     Two respondents commented on Ofgem’s proposed capital and operating
         expenditure allowances for 2005/6 and 2006/7.

2.7.     A respondent said that, taking into account that there has been an increase in the
         number of connections made, they were concerned over the significant
         difference between SP Transmission's load related capital expenditure over the
         current price control against their allowance (actual expenditure is listed as
         being over three times the allowance). Going forward, the expenditure has been
         significantly increased from £12m per annum (averaged) to £22m in 2005/6 and
         £23m pa in 2006/07. This comment also applies to SP Transmission’s non-load
         related expenditure for which the increase also has little in the way of detailed
         explanation, as well as to the increases for SHETL. The respondent suggested
         that Ofgem should consider whether a more in-depth investigation in this area is
         appropriate to ensure that these expenditures are indeed necessary, efficient and
         timely.

2.8.     SP Transmission said that they are continuously processing requests from
         renewable generators for connections to the transmission system. It said that the
         forecasts of expenditure provided to Ofgem, while based on the latest




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 14                               December 2004
            information available at the time, may therefore be superseded and it is possible
            that further expenditure will be required; this expenditure is distinct from RETS13
            expenditure, which is concerned with upgrading the transmission infrastructure
            in a GB context to accommodate the bulk transfer of energy from renewable
            generation, rather than the connection of individual generators.

2.9.        SP Transmission said that regardless of the final allowances that are agreed, it is
            important that clear mechanisms are in place for dealing with expenditure over
            and above the allowance. This could include an adjustment mechanism to
            provide additional revenue where customer requirements are significantly higher
            than previously forecast. Alternatively such additional expenditure could be
            logged up.

            Ofgem’s views

2.10.       Ofgem recognises that both SP Transmission and SHETL are increasing their
            capital expenditures to meet customer and asset performance requirements. For
            example, the companies have indicated to Ofgem that a high proportion (over
            90%) of the load related expenditure allowances relate to accepted connection
            offers or investment required to meet load growth. As discussed in the July
            report, the increase in SP Transmission’s non-load expenditure allowances is to
            fund a small number of high cost projects and the increase in SHETL’s non-load
            allowances are to fund the replacement of life expired assets where other
            remedial measures are no longer appropriate. Ofgem believes that the
            companies’ proposals for an expanded programme of new and replacement
            assets in Scotland from 2005 is reasonable, and is concerned that the capacity
            and performance that these projects are planned to deliver are fully achieved in
            the proposed timescales. Ofgem therefore proposes introducing a capital
            expenditure reporting regime to provide information on expenditure and
            delivery performance that will inform the 2007 price control review.

2.11.       Against this background, Ofgem proposes to retain the allowances for operating
            and capital expenditure set out in the draft proposals (see above). Two particular

     RETS refers to transmission investment for renewable generation.

            aspects of operating expenditure (network rates and pensions) were discussed in
            the July report.

            Network rates

2.12.       The July report recognised the uncertainties in the level of network rates for
            2005/6 and later years and that these costs are largely outside the control of
            licensees and concluded that network rates should be allowed as a pass-through
            item in the transmission price controls for SHETL and SP Transmission. SP
            Transmission welcomed the full pass-through of network rates; other
            respondents did not comment on this proposal. Ofgem believes the proposals in
            the July report remain appropriate.

            Pensions

2.13.       As discussed in the July report, SHETL has indicated that currently its
            contributions to its pension scheme are made at a rate of 20% of basic salary,
            and SP Transmission indicated that for its scheme, its normal pension costs are
            15% of gross pay. Neither the SP Transmission nor the SHETL schemes
            currently have scheme deficits.

2.14.       Ofgem concludes that no adjustments for additional pension contributions are
            considered necessary as part of the roll forward price controls for 2005/6 and
            2006/7. However if the level of company contributions should change in this
            period, this will be considered as part of the main review in 2007. In
            determining the appropriate treatment of any deficit, Ofgem will apply the same
            principles as set out in the distribution price control review (DPCR4) proposals14.

2.15.       Respondents did not comment on this proposal and Ofgem believes the
            proposals in the July report remain appropriate.


            Outputs




14
     Electricity distribution price control review, Final proposals, November 2004, Ofgem 254/04.


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment

2.16.    The output assumptions provided by SP Transmission and SHETL for the roll
        forward period were discussed in Ofgem’s July report. These output assumptions
        are re-presented in Appendix 7 to this document with some additional
        information. Ofgem notes that some of the outputs are outside the direct control
        of the licensees (eg maximum demand) but forecasting of such measures is
        important to investment decisions and changes in the forecasts can in some
        circumstances explain changes in investment decisions. Ofgem considers that
        further work is necessary to define output measures that are fully appropriate to
        transmission and this work will form part of the 2007 review.


         Rolling incentives

         Ofgem’s July report

2.17.    Ofgem’s July 2004 report discussed the introduction of rolling incentives on
        operating expenditure and capital expenditure as part of the roll forward price
        controls. Introducing rolling incentives means that efficiency savings achieved
        by companies are retained for a fixed period (5 years) instead of the retention
        period being cut short following a price control review. The effect is therefore to
        sharpen the incentive on the companies to achieve efficiency gains
        independently of the full price control period.

2.18.    The July report noted that the work on developing expenditure incentives as part
        of the DPRC4 has shown that such incentives need to be formulated with care.
        The July report also said that there are arguments for deferring consideration of
        such a scheme until the main review for 2007. These include:

         ♦        the need to define clearly the boundaries between operating expenditure
                  and capital expenditure if separate incentives on these two categories of
                  expenditure are to have the intended effect on company performance

         ♦        that the measures are proportionate to the level of periodicity in
                  operating expenditure, and

         ♦        in setting the level of expenditure allowances in the roll forward period,
                  the companies’ projections have not been subject to the in-depth
                  analysis that would be appropriate in a full review. The proposed

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 17                       December 2004
                  allowances may not therefore be the appropriate base against which to
                  measure actual performance.

2.19.    Ofgem’s July report also recognised that in the absence of a rolling mechanism,
        there is a risk that efficiency incentives may not be as strong in the roll forward
        period compared to those that would apply in a longer price control period.

         Respondents’ views

2.20.    Four respondents commented on the application of rolling expenditure
         incentives.

2.21.    A respondent said that they continued to believe that a rolling operating
         incentive may have some merit and welcomed Ofgem’s proposal to give
         consideration to stronger expenditure incentives as part of the next full review in
         2007.

2.22.    Another respondent noted that Ofgem’s June 2003 consultation document
         (Developing Monopoly Price controls: Initial conclusions) included a
         commitment to introduce a fixed retention period for efficiency savings made by
         network companies in financial years 2003/4 onwards. Regarding the discussion
         in Ofgem’s July report that the introduction of rolling incentives should be
         deferred until the next transmission review and therefore that allowances should
         be based purely upon companies’ projections, the respondent said that out-
         performance since 2003/4 may not be retained through 2005/6 and 2006/7 and
         the expected incentive for efficiency savings in the current control period is
         being removed despite the delivery of these savings. The respondent said that
         this is contradictory to previous conclusions and introduces regulatory
         uncertainty to those companies relying upon the conclusions.

2.23.    Scottish and Southern Energy (SSE) said that it had supported a simple roll
         forward of the existing price control, but consider that Ofgem has in effect
         carried out a full price review (although truncated in terms of information
         requests, etc), by re-setting the RAV and operating costs to reflect past
         expenditure and future anticipated capital expenditure and operating costs. SSE
         said that a two-year price control provides no real incentive for licensees to
         reduce costs since the resultant savings would be retained for less than two


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 18                       December 2004
           years. SSE considered that, given that there is usually an up-front investment
           required to achieve such savings and that this investment is usually equivalent to
           the annual cost saving or more, it is clear that a two-year price control is
           inconsistent with incentives for efficiency. SSE was of the view that this is
           exacerbated by the fact that Ofgem have not allowed any revenue in respect of
           the previously promised five-year rolling incentive terms for capex and opex. SSE
           stated that Ofgem’s suggestion that this will be considered in the full review in
           2007 is unacceptable.

2.24.      SSE said that if Ofgem does not propose a simple roll forward of the existing
           control then SSE believes that at the very least the capex and opex rolling
           incentive schemes as proposed in the distribution review should be applied to
           the historical capex and opex of the transmission licensees.

2.25.      SP Transmission said that although there are difficulties in establishing a rolling
           mechanism for the retention of opex savings, there is nevertheless a strong case
           for the retention of efficiency savings achieved in the current price control
           period; retention of such savings achieved since 2003/04 for a fixed period was
           a stated intention in the June 2003 paper on developing monopoly price
           controls. SP Transmission said that continuation of its current level of operating
           expenditure for the period of the price control extension would help to address
           this issue and also that of cost uncertainty relating to the introduction of BETTA.

           Ofgem’s views

2.26.      Ofgem’s June 2003 paper15 discussed the introduction of rolling incentives in
           relation to operating expenditure and capital expenditure and set out the
           approach Ofgem intended applying to all network price controls (including the
           Scottish transmission price controls) at the next relevant price control review.
           Since the purpose of the incentives is to influence licensees’ expenditure
           decisions, any incentive payments should only apply to expenditure in 2003/4
           and later years.




15
     Developing network price controls, initial conclusions, June 2003, Ofgem 54/03


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 19                              December 2004
2.27.    Ofgem therefore proposes to make rolling expenditure incentive payments in
         respect of the period 1 April 2003 to 31 March 2005. The proposed
         methodology is similar to the methodology developed as part of DPCR4, with
         payments relating to differences between actual expenditures and the
         expenditure allowances. The methodology and the resulting incentive payments
         for SP Transmission and SHETL are set out in Appendix 4.

2.28.    As regards the roll forward period (2005/6 and 2006/7), Ofgem believes that the
         arguments set out in Ofgem’s July report remain valid, and that rolling
         expenditure incentives should not apply to this period. Ofgem accepts that as a
         consequence the incentives on companies to drive for efficiency may be less
         sharp in this period. However, this potential effect is mitigated in part by
         Ofgem’s proposed capital expenditure reporting regime.

2.29.    Ofgem remains committed to the introduction of rolling incentives. However, it
         considers that the interests of consumers are best protected by considering the
         issues in depth as part of the review for 2007.


         Cost of capital

2.30.    The cost of capital is the return required by the financial markets – both debt and
         equity – to provide capital to a firm. The cost of capital is a key input when
         determining the price control for a capital intensive business. The cost of capital
         has three main components (i) the expected cost of equity; (ii) the expected cost
         of debt; and (iii) the gearing assumption.

2.31.    Ofgem’s July report proposed aligning the cost of capital for the roll forward
         transmission price controls with the outcome of the DPCR4 work on cost of
         capital.

2.32.    In addition to financing capital, the price controls need to provide funds for
         efficient levels of corporation tax payments which in part depend on the cost of




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 20                       December 2004
            capital assumptions16. The DPCR4 work investigated the appropriate level of tax
            allowances.

            Respondents’ views

2.33.       Four respondents commented on the proposed approach to cost of capital.

2.34.       A respondent said that they do not see the need for any change to the cost of
            capital for the roll forward price controls unless the DPCR4 work indicates the
            need for significantly different values from those currently in use.

2.35.       Noting that cost allowances have been calculated using a pre-tax cost of capital
            of 6.5%, and that consideration will be given to adjusting this once work on the
            cost of capital for the electricity distribution companies is completed, another
            respondent said that in light of the changes to taxation from 2005 it would be
            more appropriate to set the extended price controls on a post-tax basis.

2.36.       SSE said that they regard the present proposal of 6.5% pre-tax (which is
            equivalent to 4.6% post-tax real) as unacceptable. SSE considered that in
            previous reviews there existed scope for significant out-performance by efficient
            companies but that such scope for out-performance is more limited at this
            review. SSE welcomed the commitment to apply the same cost of capital as
            finally emerges from the distribution price control review to the transmission
            price control.

2.37.       SP Transmission said that the returns of electricity network companies should be
            at least consistent with those of water companies. SP Transmission said that the
            treatment of tax for the transmission companies must be consistent with the
            precedent set in the distribution price control review, that is, the cost of capital
            should be calculated on a post-tax basis with company specific allowances for
            tax costs. On this basis, the allowed cost of capital should be at least 5.1% fully
            post-tax.

            Ofgem’s views




16
     Financing issues are discussed in more detail in the distribution price control review proposals.


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 21                                        December 2004
2.38.    At the previous SP Transmission and SHETL reviews, Ofgem adopted a pre-tax
         cost of capital. The allowed cost of capital under the existing price controls (on a
         pre-tax real basis) is 6.5 per cent. In the pre-tax approach, Ofgem provided for
         tax liabilities through an allowance in the pre-tax cost of capital.

2.39.    In the distribution price control review (DPCR4), Ofgem used a post-tax
         approach to the cost of capital. Ofgem decided to use a post-tax approach
         (calculating tax allowances separately) for DPCR4, in order to:

         ♦        reflect the change to the Inland Revenue’s treatment of network capital
                  expenditure, which is expected to increase effective tax rates for most
                  companies;

         ♦        improve consistency with other aspects of the regulatory framework, in
                  which changes in the level of costs are passed on to consumers at the
                  subsequent price control review; and

         ♦        reduce the incentives to increase gearing.

2.40.    In principle, Ofgem considers that these objectives apply equally to SP
         Transmission and SHETL in the setting of their price controls.

2.41.    When a post-tax cost of capital is being used the price control calculations
         require specific estimates of tax costs plus a pre-tax return on debt and a post-tax
         return on equity. The cost of capital assumptions used in the distribution price
         control review proposals17 were as follows:



          Key components                                %
          Cost of debt (pre-tax)                       4.1
          Cost of equity (post-tax)                    7.5
          Gearing                                      57.5


2.42.    These assumptions give the post-tax cost of capital of 4.8% used in DPCR4.
         Furthermore, in DPCR4, Ofgem derived tax allowances for each company from
         a notional balance sheet with the gearing level assumed equal to that used in the




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 22                        December 2004
            cost of capital calculation (i.e. 57.5%), and a corporation tax rate of 30% applied
            to Ofgem’s forecast of profits chargeable to corporation tax. The assumptions
            used to calculate the tax allowances were based on Ofgem’s review of the
            distribution companies’ 2002/3 corporation tax computations and forecast tax
            costs.

2.43.       In terms of the underlying factors affecting the cost of capital, an analysis carried
            out as part of the price control reviews in 1999 found no conclusive evidence
            that the Scottish transmission companies were, in financial terms, significantly
            different from the electricity distribution companies. Given the similarity in
            transmission and distribution activities, Ofgem does not consider that different
            cost of capital considerations should apply to the Scottish transmission
            companies compared to the electricity distribution companies.

2.44.       Ofgem therefore proposes to carry out an alignment of the cost of capital for SP
            Transmission and SHETL to that proposed for the DPCR4 final proposals.

2.45.       An approach considered by Ofgem for SP Transmission and SHETL was to
            calculate the post-tax cost of capital as in DPCR4 and then calculate tax
            allowances specific to each company. However, this would have involved a
            detailed and comprehensive examination of the companies’ tax calculations
            which has not been possible in this roll forward review, and it is considered that
            there is the risk that moving directly to a post tax cost of capital without such
            analysis may unduly disadvantage consumers or licensees.

2.46.       It is therefore not considered appropriate to reach a conclusion on the price
            controls for SP Transmission and SHETL calculated on a full post-tax basis. It is
            instead proposed to carry out the alignment of the costs of capital for SP
            Transmission and SHETL with DPCR4 by using data from the DPCR4 proposals
            to calculate an equivalent pre-tax cost of capital that takes into account the level
            of tax allowances proposed as part of the DPCR4 final proposals. It is therefore
            proposed to apply an equivalent pre-tax cost of capital in calculating the allowed
            revenues of SP Transmission and SHETL.

         management and likely to have similar approaches to accounting and tax
         policies.

         Financial indicators

2.53.    In setting the price controls, Ofgem needs to have regard to its principal
         objective and general duties and in particular to have regard to the need for
         ensuring that companies can finance their activities. Also, Ofgem’s July paper
         has indicated that it intends to propose price controls that are consistent with
         companies maintaining credit ratings that are comfortably within the investment
         grade range. As part of the distribution price control review, Ofgem considered
         “financeability” by calculating and assessing certain financial ratios that are used
         by credit rating agencies and others in the financial community to assess the
         financial strength of a company.




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 25                                   December 2004
2.54.      For three important indicators, conservative test values, which would be
           consistent with credit ratings comfortably within investment grade, were set out
           in DPCR418:

           ♦         funds flow from operation (FFO) / interest not less than 3x

           ♦         retained cashflow to debt not less than 9%, and

           ♦         debt to RAV not higher than 65%

2.55.      Ofgem believes that the Scottish transmission companies are exposed to a
           similar level of risk as the distribution companies, and expects the transmission
           companies to be able to support a similar level of gearing as the distribution
           companies and thus exhibit similar performance in terms of financial ratios. The
           target financial ratios for SP Transmission and SHETL have therefore been taken
           to equal those used in the DPCR4 proposals, and are assumed to apply to the
           Scottish transmission companies as to the distribution companies.

2.56.      High level financial models for SP Transmission and SHETL show that with the
           proposed levels of allowed revenues for the roll forward period, both companies
           are comfortably within the targets.

           completion schedules.

           monitoring arrangements for the roll forward period, and given that there has
           been no detailed review of the capex proposals, this would seem reasonable.
           Noting Ofgem's intention to introduce a monitoring regime around capital

Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 27                       December 2004
     3. England-Scotland interconnector regulatory
        asset values
3.1.     Ofgem’s May report explained that under BETTA the England-Scotland
         interconnector assets are to be incorporated into the transmission systems and
         therefore into the price controls of the respective transmission licensees. This
         involves adding appropriate values to the respective licensees’ RAVs. The July
         report discussed Ofgem’s proposal to use a market-based valuation and the
         issues that needed to be addressed in coming to a valuation decision.

3.2.     This chapter summarises respondents’ views on the issues raised in the July
         report and presents Ofgem’s proposed valuation approach and the associated
         values for the relevant interconnector assets.

3.3.     In making proposals to adjust the transmission licensees’ price controls for the
         England-Scotland interconnector at BETTA go-live, Ofgem is assuming that no
         termination amounts or ongoing liabilities are enforced between the parties to
         the UIA and the SIA in relation to these contracts. If termination or ongoing
         payments do apply, Ofgem will need to consider why these may be appropriate
         and whether it needs to review its proposals in relation to the England-Scotland
         interconnector valuations.


         Background

3.4.     Ofgem’s March19 and May20 reports set out in detail the contractual and
         regulatory arrangements related to charges for use of the England-Scotland
         interconnector.

3.5.     At Vesting, the interconnector capacity was 850MW. This is the pre-Vesting
         interconnector capacity. Since Vesting there have been two upgrades. The first,
         completed in 1993, increased the nominal capacity to 1600MW, and the



19
  Review of transmission price controls from 2005: SP Transmission Ltd, Scottish Hydro-Electric
Transmission Ltd, Initial thoughts, March 2004, Ofgem 52/04




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 28                                  December 2004
           second, completed in 2003, increased the nominal capacity to 2200MW. The
           upgrades together comprise the post-Vesting capacity.

3.6.       As discussed in the March 2004 report, the England-Scotland interconnector
           comprises sections located in England & Wales (the NGC interconnector) and
           sections located in Scotland (the Scottish interconnection). The NGC
           interconnector is owned by NGC’s interconnectors business, but is operated and
           maintained on its behalf by NGC’s transmission business. The Scottish
           interconnection is owned, maintained and operated by SP Transmission. SHETL
           has certain rights in recognition of the contributions made by SHETL to capital
           and operating costs. SP Transmission and SHETL provide access to their
           individual shares of the total interconnector capacity to parties requesting transit.

3.7.       The May 2004 report explained that pre-Vesting assets in the Scottish
           interconnection are currently funded through the price controls of SP
           Transmission and SHETL but that the post-Vesting interconnector assets
           provided to upgrade the capacity of the interconnector are outside the controls.
           All of NGC’s interconnector assets, both the pre-Vesting and the post-Vesting
           upgrade capacity are outside their price control (although the charges from NGC
           to the Scottish companies in relation to their pre-Vesting assets are within the
           two Scottish price controls)..

3.8.       Ofgem’s March report described the current contract arrangements. SP
           Transmission and SHETL have a contract with NGC for use of NGC’s
           interconnector capacity21. These contractual arrangements allow NGC to charge
           SP Transmission and SHETL for use of all of its England-Scotland interconnector
           assets. However, all the costs incurred by SP Transmission and SHETL in
           relation to the pre-Vesting interconnector assets in both England and Scotland
           are recovered through their respective transmission system charges. The costs of
           the post-Vesting interconnector upgrades are recovered by SP Transmission and
           SHETL directly from interconnector users.

3.9.       Pre-BETTA, the basis for setting charges for use of the upgrades to the
           interconnector in Scotland is set out in the Scottish transmission companies’

20
     Transmission price controls and BETTA: Update, May 2004, Ofgem 107/04



Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 29                          December 2004
         licences; essentially charges for use of the interconnector are required to be set
         at a level which will enable the licensee to recover no more than a reasonable
         rate of return on the capital represented by the interconnector assets. Similar
         considerations apply to NGC in setting its charges for use of the interconnector
         in England. In 2001, Ofgem concluded that in relation to the interconnector, a
         10% real rate of return on upgrade assets was reasonable22, taking into account
         the exposure to project risk of the transmission licensees whilst the
         interconnector upgrades remain outside the transmission price controls.

3.10.    Under BETTA, the interconnector assets on both sides of the border will become
         part of the transmission systems of the licensees who currently own the assets
         (NGC and SP Transmission). Financing of the assets will be provided through
         adjustments to the price controlled revenues of the relevant companies rather
         than through the interconnector contracts currently in place.

3.11.    This requires an estimate of the value of the interconnector assets to be added to
         the licensees’ RAVs. Ofgem’s May paper considered different ways of valuing
         the England-Scotland interconnector and the July paper proposed adopting a
         market-based value calculated as the present value of the expected net revenue
         stream post BETTA go-live. The proposed approach for deriving the market-based
         value was to calculate the present value (at a 10% discount rate) of the net
         revenue streams expected to be received over the period from 1 April 2005 for
         the planned life of the assets. The report also highlighted a number of possible
         adjustments taking into account:

         ♦        the fact that the contract termination arrangements do not deliver the
                  present value of future cashflows;

         ♦        the appropriate treatment of availability incentive payments23;

         ♦        the shortfall in revenues in the period up to 2005 to deliver a 10%
                  return; and

   Use of Interconnector Agreement (Scotland)

   Scotland-England Interconnector: Access & charging principles to 31 March 2002 and access principles to

   The availability payments provide an incentive for NGC and SP Transmission, the operators of the
