         Responses to Ofgem’s July report

3.12.    Six respondents commented on the proposed method of valuation and the
        factors to be considered.

3.13.    A respondent said that they had some concerns with the use of net cashflows for
        valuing the interconnector. They said that some analysis should be undertaken in
        relation to the level of bookings of capacity and the actual utilisation of such
        capacity; this is to ensure the correct balance of risk and value is represented by
        the valuation.

3.14.    Another respondent said that a market based valuation would result in a higher
        level of return for these assets than for other transmission assets, whereas the
        commercial risk previously associated with these assets has been considerably
        reduced, and said that these lower risks should be factored into the calculation
        of the market based value such that transmission users are not required to pay for
        a significantly higher rate of return on these assets than for other comparable
        transmission assets.

3.15.    A further respondent said that there is a fundamental difference between the
        existing interconnector arrangements under which the asset owners invested in
        the assets in the full knowledge of their exposure to significant risks (e.g. of
        availability and utilisation) and incorporation of these assets into the price
        controls, whereby cost recovery will essentially be guaranteed, socialised and
        independent of the utilisation of the assets. Referring to Ofgem’s proposal to
        adopt market based valuation, the respondent that there is no justification for the
        valuation of the assets to exceed RAV-based figures; any valuation in excess of
        RAV risks imposing stranded costs on transmission users. Ofgem's indicative
        market valuation is more than 2.5 times the book value of the assets, and also
        well above the estimated modern equivalent asset (MEA) value, and would seem
        to deliver windfall gains to the TOs (and hence windfall losses to consumers).
        The respondent proposed that any valuation in excess of RAV should be ring-
        fenced within the price controls and recovered from the demand side. It also

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 31                       December 2004
        said that is not obvious why estimates of MEA should be more uncertain than the
        hypothetical market-based estimates which Ofgem is proposing to use, noting
        that capacity auctions on the England-France Interconnector have shown
        substantial price volatility, underlining the uncertainty attached to any market-
        based assessment of the value of interconnector assets.

3.16.    SSE agreed that SHETL’s investment in the interconnector upgrades should be
        compensated by adding an appropriate value to SHETL’s RAV and that each
        licensee should have its RAV adjusted to allow for their investment in
        interconnector assets. SSE said it will be necessary to ensure that there is
        consistent treatment of interconnector investments across all licensees in terms
        of implied returns – it therefore believed that the addition to the RAV should
        reflect the present value of the contracts subject to a reasonableness test of a
        10% rate of return on the assets involved.

3.17.    SP Transmission said that a market-based approach for valuing the
        Interconnector should be used; this approach is consistent with SP
        Transmission’s case for increasing the Regulatory Asset Value by the net present
        value of outstanding revenues under current commercial arrangements.
        Concerning the factors identified by Ofgem that may affect the calculation of a
        market based value, SP Transmission said the following:

         ♦        Incentive payments under the contract that increase or decrease
                  according to delivered availability: SP Transmission said that the
                  availability adjustment is part of its forecast revenue streams under
                  current arrangements and that its continued inclusion in the net present
                  value of outstanding revenues is reasonable.

         ♦        Pre-and post-BETTA cashflows: Referring to Ofgem’s view that the risks
                  borne by the licensees after incorporation of the assets into the price
                  control are less than under the current arrangements and that these lower
                  risks should be factored into the calculation of the market value, SP
                  Transmission said that, as a consequence of the present commercial
                  arrangements it had not yet been fully compensated for the risk already
                  taken in respect of the upgrade investments; therefore a valuation



Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 32                        December 2004
                  approach based on the present value of the full outstanding revenue
                  streams that are still to accrue to SP Transmission should be carried out.

3.18.    National Grid Transco (NGT) welcomed Ofgem’s proposal to use a market-based
        approach to determine the regulatory value of the interconnector assets and
        agreed with the proposal to use a discount rate of 10% to determine the present
        value of expected contract cash flows. Concerning the factors identified by
        Ofgem that may affect the calculation of a market based value NGT said the
        following:

         ♦        Contract termination payments: The contract termination amounts
                  payable by the Scottish companies to NGC may represent a lower bound
                  on the market value of the NGC section of the interconnector. In this
                  worst-case outcome, the beneficiary of the contract would not receive
                  any value arising from on going out-performance of the assumptions
                  made during the negotiation of the contract. In terms of the effect of the
                  existence of the termination clause on the market value of the
                  interconnector contract NGT said that given the importance of the
                  interconnector in terms of providing a market for generation in Scotland,
                  its expectation is that, in the absence of BETTA, the Scottish companies
                  would not wish to terminate the contract. Moreover, given the expected
                  development of significant new renewable generation in Scotland, it is
                  likely that, even if it were terminated, other companies would wish to
                  enter a similar agreement to maintain the interconnector capacity (or
                  even upgrade it). On this basis, given the low probability of termination
                  and also the strong likelihood that an alternative contract could be
                  negotiated, the termination amounts should not have a material impact
                  on the market value of the interconnector assets.

         ♦        Incentive payments under the contract that increase or decrease
                  according to delivered availability: NGT said that the expected future
                  cash flows assume that the availability of the interconnector circuits
                  remains at a target level that corresponds with the availability of the
                  generality of other transmission system circuits. NGT noted that the
                  outturn availability achieved on the interconnector circuits has actually


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 33                        December 2004
                  been somewhat higher, and that it should retain the value from
                  outperforming the contract assumptions.

         ♦        Pre-and post-BETTA cashflows: NGT said that the actual and forecast
                  cash flows and revenues for the period up to April 2005 and those
                  beyond April 2005 (assuming that the pre- BETTA arrangements
                  continue), reflect the fixed price nature of the interconnector contract,
                  and this revenue profile does not give a constant rate of return over time
                  but relies on revenues to be received in later years to achieve the desired
                  lifetime rate of return. NGT said that the market-based valuation of the
                  interconnector should consider only those cash flows that would arise
                  under the pre-BETTA arrangements and not reflect any benefits that
                  might arise from these assets under BETTA (e.g. from a post-BETTA SO
                  incentive scheme). It said that under the pre-BETTA arrangements these
                  circuits have not given rise to constraint payments because the
                  contractual framework limits use of the interconnector to just the
                  interconnector capacity available, and there has not been a pre-BETTA
                  benefit under its SO incentive scheme that needs to be included in the
                  valuation methodology.


         Ofgem’s views

3.19.    This section sets out:

         ♦        the key criteria on which Ofgem’s decision in relation to the appropriate
                  valuation of the interconnector assets is based;

         ♦        Ofgem’s views on the issues raised in the July report relating to
                  adjustments to market value; and

         ♦        Ofgem’s proposed valuation methodology and proposed interconnector
                  values.

        Key decision criteria

3.20.    Under BETTA, the interconnector contracts will cease to have effect and the
        financing of the interconnector will be through the price controls. The financial


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 34                        December 2004
        arrangements under the price controls must be consistent with the Authority’s
        statutory duties. It is also important that the proposed arrangements are
        consistent with, among other things, Article 1 of the First Protocol of the
        European Convention on Human Rights (right to peaceful enjoyment of
        possessions).

3.21.    Ofgem recognises that investment in large infrastructure projects relies on
        certainty over the regulatory framework within which the investments are made,
        Ofgem therefore considers that the introduction of BETTA should not of itself be
        detrimental to the return which investors in the England-Scotland interconnector
        could reasonably have expected.

3.22.    However, the transfer value also needs to take into account the extent to which
        the existing interconnector contracts embody any return in excess of a
        reasonable level given the risks involved in the project, in order that such excess
        return is not “locked in” to the price control revenues in relation to the
        interconnector.

        Factors raised in July report.

3.23.    As noted above, Ofgem’s July report proposed adopting the market-based value,
        with a number of possible adjustments where appropriate to take into account
        factors including:

         ♦        the fact that the contract termination arrangements do not deliver the
                  present value of future cashflows

         ♦        the appropriate treatment of the availability incentive payments, and

         ♦        the shortfall in revenues in the period up to 2005 to deliver a 10%
                  return; and

         ♦        whether projected contract cashflows are believed to provide a return in
                  excess of a reasonable level.




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 35                       December 2004
3.24.      In relation to contract termination payments, Ofgem believes that, coupled with
           a view on the revenue which NGC24 might secure through any subsequent
           contract, the level of the contract termination payments might provide a
           reasonable estimate of the value of the assets. NGC have said that there is a low
           probability of termination and also that there is a strong likelihood that an
           alternative contract could be negotiated with another party.

3.25.      However, any judgement in relation to the level of revenues under a subsequent
           contract is extremely subjective. It is not clear that another party purchasing
           interconnector capacity from NGC would negotiate an agreement which
           reflected the current contract. Given this subjectivity, Ofgem do not believe the
           termination payments should form the basis of valuation of NGC’s
           interconnector assets.

3.26.      In relation to availability incentive payments, in the past both NGC and SP
           Transmission have managed to achieve high asset availability levels and
           therefore have received incentive payments under the interconnector
           arrangements. These availability payments have been in place since the
           interconnector contracts were agreed, but contract changes mean that they are a
           less important part of future revenue streams. Going forward, only SP
           Transmission forecast receipt of availability incentive payments.

3.27.      The licence condition governing the return on the interconnector relates solely
           to the absolute return earned by the companies, and does not distinguish
           between charging elements or the levels of certainty associated with them.
           Therefore, Ofgem believes availability incentives should simply be considered as
           a component of overall revenue which is subject to the “reasonable rate of
           return” test.

3.28.      In relation to the shortfall in revenue in revenues in the period up to 2005, it is
           true that as a result of the accounting treatment applied by the companies in
           setting interconnector charges, the actual rate of return earned by the companies
           will vary from year to year.




24
     There are no termination payment provisions in the contracts between SPTL and SHETL and


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 36                                 December 2004
3.29.    The contract revenue profiles were set so that the charges were constant over
         time in real terms. This does not give a constant rate of return over time. The
         desired lifetime rate of return (given that some investments in upgrade capacity
         have been incurred fairly recently) therefore relies partly on revenues to be
         received in later years, that is post-BETTA go-live.

3.30.    Ofgem therefore agrees that the assessment of a reasonable rate of return should
         take this variation into account.

3.31.    Finally, in relation to whether projected contract cashflows are believed to
         provide a return in excess of a reasonable level, the following table shows the
         implied rates of return over the lifetime of the project:

                                    Project lifetime return
          NGC                       12.5%
          SP Transmission           12.5%
          SHETL                     8%



3.32.    There are a number of possible benchmarks for a reasonable rate of return given
         the risks involved over the lifetime of the project:

         ♦        Ofgem have previously stated that a return of 10% could be considered
                  reasonable . This rate of return on assets was proposed by SP
                  Transmission in the interconnector charge setting process in 2001 –
                  Ofgem noted that they had reviewed it and “concluded that it is
                  reasonable, whilst interconnector upgrades remain outside the
                  transmission price controls”; and

         ♦        SHETL’s contracts embody a lower rate of return than those of the other
                  companies. This could be argued to set a benchmark on the basis that
                  SHETL was willing to make the investments with this rate of return
                  (although SSE have argued that the investment was made on the basis of
                  returns to the group as a whole, and that the relatively lower
                  transmission business return was offset by a relatively higher earnings in
                  their generation business). Equally, the post-tax return in the recent


interconnector users, although contract notice periods do apply.


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 37                       December 2004
                  DPCR4 proposals is equivalent to an average of an 8% pre-tax return
                  across distribution businesses. Since ownership of distribution assets is
                  less risky than ownership of the interconnector, SHETL’s contract returns
                  could be argued to set an absolute minimum level of a reasonable return.

3.33.    Ofgem continue to believe that a return of 10% should be considered
        reasonable for the interconnector assets while they remain outside the price
        controls. This is the rate of return deemed by Ofgem to be ‘reasonable’ in
        respect of the most recent investment to upgrade capacity on the
        interconnector25. This is, therefore, the return that might reasonably have been
        expected in the absence of BETTA.

        Valuation approach

3.34.    Ofgem does not believe that respondents’ arguments against a market value
        approach are well founded:

         ♦        while it is true that interconnector ownership will be less risky post-
                  BETTA, the value that the companies are giving up in return for the RAB
                  transfer relates to the current contractual framework. The key question is
                  therefore the appropriateness of the return given a reasonable view of
                  project risk under the current contracts. Is it a reasonable return, given
                  the current framework which the companies should expect to retain
                  (albeit in the future through a stream of regulated income); and

         ♦        there should be no windfall gain in a transfer at a fair assessment of
                  market value, since the value of the transfer should already be implicit in
                  the contracts to which the companies are party.

3.35.    Ofgem continues to believe that a market value based approach is appropriate.
        This approach involves valuing the interconnector at the Net Present Value of
        the future contract cashflows (i.e. contract revenue less both investments and
        maintenance and operating costs).




25
  Scotland-England Interconnector: Access & charging principles to 31 March 2002 and access principles to
31 March 2004, A final proposals and consultation document, Ofgem, December 2001.


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 38                                 December 2004
3.36.    The current interconnector contracts were not established in the context of
        competitive market conditions. Some of the contracts are the outcome of a
        negotiation between the monopoly transmission companies in Scotland and in
        England & Wales. Equally, in relation to the contracts with users, the Scottish
        transmission companies could be argued to have had a captive generation
        market interested in purchasing interconnector rights.

3.37.    Given this context for the negotiation of the contracts, and since the returns
        embodied in the existing contracts appear, for two of the companies, to exceed a
        10% benchmark for a reasonable rate, it may be appropriate, in calculating the
        transfer value, to make an adjustment to the assumed contract cashflows to
        ensure that return in excess of a benchmark reasonable level is not “locked in” to
        the price control.

3.38.    To take into account the variations in the profile of return under the contracts
        from year to year, contract revenues for each year of the project could be
        adjusted such that, over the period 1989/90 to 2033/34, they would provide for
        a 10% rate of return overall. The transfer value could then be calculated as the
        present value of the adjusted revenues and contract costs from 2005/6 to
        2033/34 at a 10% discount rate (reflecting the implied cost of capital related to
        interconnector ownership).

3.39.    Under this approach, there would be no adjustments to revenues already
        accrued to the companies. Ofgem believes that to make adjustments to correct
        for excess returns retrospectively would not be consistent with a stable
        regulatory climate for investment, and hence would be inappropriate.

3.40.    In SHETL’s case, the above approach would involve some upward revision to
        contract revenues. This could be argued to be appropriate, as under BETTA,
        SSE’s generation business will have to pay for network access based on charges
        which reflect, among other things, the overall return on all parties’
        interconnector assets. If SHETL continue to earn 8% and the other companies
        earn 10%, SSE as a group would, other things being equal, be worse off as a
        result of the change in the treatment of interconnector assets (SHETL would
        continue to earn their current return but SSE’s generation business would face
        higher charges).


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 39                        December 2004
3.41.    An alternative to the approach described above would be to calculate the
        transfer as the present value (using the 10% discount rate) of actual contract
        costs and revenues (i.e. leaving contract revenues unadjusted for returns above
        the 10% level).

3.42.    Ofgem does not believe it would appropriate to use a 6.5% discount rate in
        either of these approaches, as the discount rate should reflect the cost of capital
        of interconnector asset ownership under the current contractual framework
        rather than under the price controls.

3.44.    In coming to a decision on the valuation for the interconnector, Ofgem has
        considered the key criteria set out above. It has considered the context for the
        negotiation of the contracts, the rates of return implied by the current contract
        cashflows (against a benchmark for a reasonable rate of return of 10% reflecting
        the riskiness of the project), and the licence requirement to earn no more than a
        reasonable rate of return. Taking these considerations into account, Ofgem
        proposes to adopt a transfer value based on the adjusted revenues in the table
        above.

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 41                       December 2004
     4. BETTA price controls
4.1.     From 26th August 2004 to 1st September 2004 the legal framework for the
         British Electricity Trading and Transmission Arrangements (BETTA) was
         introduced. Amongst other things the Secretary of State:

         i)   made a licensing scheme for each of the existing electricity transmission
              licences held by NGC, SP Transmission and SHETL

         ii) determined new standard conditions for electricity transmission licences

         iii) modified existing special conditions for electricity transmission licences, and

         iv) designated the SO -TO Code (STC) and changes to other codes26.

4.2.     Certain aspects of this legal framework are now in effect, and other aspects will
         take effect on and from the BETTA go-live date.

4.3.     Under this framework, NGC is the GB system operator, in addition to owning
         transmission assets in England and Wales. SP Transmission and SHETL are
         transmission owners in their respective areas of Scotland.

4.4.     This chapter sets out Ofgem’s final proposals for the price controls to apply
         under BETTA, expressed as adjustments to the price controls that would apply in
         the absence of BETTA. It is proposed that the adjustments necessary as a result of
         BETTA will be made by adding additional terms to the basic price control
         formula for each licensee.

4.5.     Changes are necessary for all three transmission licensees. This chapter presents
         the proposed values for the adjustments to the price controls for SP Transmission
         and SHETL for the years 2005/6 and 2006/7, and for the adjustments to NGC’s
         TO27 and SO internal controls for 2005/628 as a result of BETTA.




26
   CUSC, BSC and Grid Code
27
   The terms TO and SO used in the context of NGC’s price controls are different from the terms SO and TO
used the context of the STC under BETTA.
28
   NGC’s current internal cost price controls are intended to last until 31 March 2006, and the price controls


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 42                                     December 2004
4.6.     This chapter sets out Ofgem’s proposed form of control under BETTA and its
         final price control proposals29.

4.7.     Under BETTA, the England-Scotland interconnector is to be incorporated into the
         price controls of the relevant licensees. Chapter 3 sets out Ofgem’s proposals for
         values to use for the relevant interconnector assets at BETTA go-live and these
         values are used in setting the opening RAVs in the price control adjustments set
         out in this chapter.


         Price control adjustments for BETTA

4.8.     To adjust the price controls for BETTA, the changes to operating expenditure,
         capital expenditure and the Regulatory Asset Values (RAV) as a result of BETTA
         need to be considered. The combined impact of these changes on the allowed
         revenues for each year is then calculated for each company. The base levels
         against which these changes have been developed are:

         ♦        for SP Transmission and SHETL, the roll forward price controls for
                  2005/6 and 2006/7 set out in Chapter 3, and

         ♦        for NGC, the existing price controls applying to 2005/6.

4.9.     As in the July report, Ofgem considers it appropriate for the cost of capital to
         apply to the BETTA adjustments to be the same as that applying to the main
         controls of the respective companies. Ofgem has made proposals for the pre-tax
         cost of capital to apply under the roll forward controls for SP Transmission and
         SHETL in chapter 2, and these values are applied in this chapter. For NGC, a
         6.25% cost of capital is assumed, in line with the assumption made in setting
         their existing price controls.

         Operating cost adjustments

         Ofgem’s July report




to apply to NGC for 2006/7 are being reviewed separately.

   As discussed in Appendix1, NGC’s SO external cost incentive from 1 April 2005 is being taken forward

4.10.    Ofgem proposed changes to operating cost allowances resulting from the
         introduction of BETTA. The changes are:

         ♦        to provide for the BETTA implementation costs incurred by transmission
                  licensees

         ♦        to provide for change to ongoing costs because of the changes in roles
                  under BETTA. Ofgem proposed certain values for network rates, licence
                  fees, and for the aggregate cost of outage rescheduling at short notice for
                  each licensee.

         ♦        to fund the England-Scotland interconnector as part the price controls,
                  and

         ♦        to fund the additional transmission system assets resulting from the
                  introduction of the PLUGS connection charging methodology.

         Respondents’ views

4.11.    Two respondents commented on the operating cost changes.

4.12.    Regarding the licence fees and network rates that NGC will pay as a result of all
         electricity transmission related licence fees being paid by the GBSO and the
         inclusion of the interconnector, NGT noted that both of these items are treated
         as pass-through within the existing NGC price control formula and therefore no
         adjustment to the formula is required in respect of these items.

4.13.    NGT noted that Ofgem had used its lowest estimate of outage rescheduling
         costs, based on NGC’s experience within England & Wales, and further noted a
         number of factors which could affect these allowances including the
         consequences of changes by one TO on another. NGC suggested that it would
         be appropriate to consider the introduction of a specific income adjusting event
         term so that an adjustment could be made if the costs of Scottish outage
         adjustments prove to be significantly higher than was expected at the time the
         price control was set.

4.14.    Regarding the £1m that Ofgem has been deducted to account for an assumed
         income for rearranging outages, SSE said that as a general rule, outages are not

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 44                       December 2004
         rearranged at short notice and SSE have not included any costs in the roll
         forward business plan for rearranging outages; any income received from the TO
         would simply equate to these additional costs, since the obligation is to submit
         cost reflective charges for rearranging outages.

         Ofgem’s proposals

4.15.    Ofgem notes NGC’s comment on the licence modifications regarding the pass-
         through of network rates and licence fees and will take these into account in the
         development of the draft licence conditions presented in chapter 5.

4.16.    Ofgem has considered the information provided by licensees on outage
         rescheduling costs. SP Transmission and SHETL have indicated that current
         arrangements in Scotland mean that the current number of occasions where
         outages are rescheduled (for reasons equivalent to those under BETTA where
         they would be remunerated by NGC) are few, and these have a very low cost.
         Ofgem has not therefore made adjustments to SP Transmission’s or SHETL’s
         operating expenditure allowances for these costs. Based on their experience of
         operating in England and Wales, NGC has indicated that under BETTA they
         believe transmission owners in Scotland may make charges for outage
         rescheduling which could amount to £10m pa in some circumstances.

4.17.    Ofgem accepts the information licensees have provided on costs incurred under
         current arrangements, but is not able to establish firmly the costs that will apply
         under BETTA. Ofgem therefore proposes retaining the expenditure allowance for
         NGC (£3m, the lower end of the range of estimated costs) in respect of outage
         rescheduling costs incurred with transmission owners. However, Ofgem
         proposes to introduce an income adjusting event arrangement in NGC’s licence
         to reflect the present uncertainty in these costs. The income adjusting event
         arrangement would provide for NGC’s revenues to be adjusted up or down in
         the event that the outage rescheduling costs with transmission owners are
         significantly different30 from £3m pa.

         BETTA implementation costs

4.18.       Ofgem’s July report proposed that BETTA implementation costs classified as
            operating expenditure should be recovered by licensees over a 2 year period
            (2005/6 and 2006/7). A seven year period was proposed for recovery of capital
            expenditure31. Since the capital expenditure component of the implementation
            costs incurred by SP Transmission and SHETL are small, it is proposed that in the
            case of SP Transmission and SHETL, all implementation costs (opex and capex)
            should be recovered over the two year period. For NGC, the implementation
            costs include a significant capital component (approx £18m) incurred prior to
            go-live and it is proposed to retain separate recovery arrangements. The
            projected expenditures of licensees on BETTA implementation are set out in
            Appendix 8 for SP Transmission, Appendix 9 for SHETL and in Appendix 10 for
            NGC.

          Financial indicators

 4.35.    As regards SP Transmission and SHETL, Ofgem has reviewed the financial
          indicators discussed in chapter 2 to take into account the price control
          adjustments under BETTA proposed in this chapter. The results indicate that for
          both companies the financial ratios remain within the target values.

          this chapter are of sufficient magnitude to warrant a formal review of the
          financial indicators appropriate to NGC.


          Forms of control

          Ofgem’s July report

 4.37.    The July report proposed adjusting the price controls of each licensee for specific
          elements of cost arising as a result of BETTA. The additional terms in the price
          control formulae are for the following factors:

          ♦         BETTA implementation costs (BIt term), and

 Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
 Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 50                               December 2004
4.38.    Changes to SP Transmission and SHETL’s revenue restrictions are also proposed
        to allow for revised connection costs resulting from any change to the boundary
        between connections and infrastructure assets (CCt term).

4.39.    Ofgem’s July report proposed that transmission owners should have a licence
        obligation to notify NGC according to a specified schedule of the amounts
        representing defined components of the revenues they propose to recover in
        respect of a year. This arrangement allows certainty to NGC over the level of its
        allowed revenues and allows NGC to calculate its charges to users in a timely
        fashion.

4.40.    The July report also proposed the introduction of an income adjusting event
        provision to all transmission licences to apply, if appropriate, when changes are
        made to the STC (the IATt term).

         Respondents’ views

4.41.    Three respondents commented on the proposed forms of control.

4.42.    A respondent agreed with Ofgem’s proposed approach to the setting of revenue
         restrictions for the two Scottish Transmission Owners and NGC under BETTA
         and the proposed revenue flows between NGC and transmission owners.

4.43.    SSE agreed that the form of revenue restrictions should be as the existing
         condition, plus the additional terms specified in the consultation to cover BETTA
         implementation (BIt), change of role (ECt), connection boundary effects (CCt),
         and income adjusting events (IATt) (if any). SSE said that the CCt term relates to
         assets that have been classified as connection pre-BETTA, but which will
         become infrastructure post BETTA as a result of the introduction of the GB
         transmission charging methodology and said that the same approach should be
         adopted as for NGC. This would involve a licence modification to adjust the
         allowed revenue by a depreciation and rate of return element on any “legacy”
         assets.

4.44.    NGT agreed with the proposed form of NGC’s revenue restrictions under BETTA
         and the proposed codification in licence conditions, but regarding the TSPC and
         TSHC terms which are to be set to zero initially, NGT said it would welcome

         clarification concerning the circumstances that Ofgem believe may arise that
         would justify the inclusion of such terms.

         Ofgem’s final proposals

         proposals, Ofgem considers that these proposals remain appropriate. The
         following sections set out the additional terms it is proposed to add to the basic
         price control formula for each licensee.

         SP Transmission and SHETL

        the formula for calculating the maximum allowed revenues for SP Transmission

         ♦        BIt , the allowance for BETTA implementation costs

         ♦        ECt, the adjustment for allowed revenues consequent on the change in
                  role to that of transmission owner (including incorporation of the
                  England-Scotland interconnector into the price control)

         ♦        CCt , the adjustment for capital contribution repayments

         ♦        IATt, the amount of any allowed income adjustment in respect of the
                  relevant period t (related to STC changes).

Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 57                                   December 2004
   5. Draft licence modifications
5.1.     Ofgem’s July report set out the existing special conditions for each licensee
        related to the revenue restrictions. This chapter sets out Ofgem’s proposals for
        the draft licence modifications that implement Ofgem’s final proposals for each
        licensee.

5.2.    The July report set out initial drafting for the changes necessary for BETTA,
        including:

         ♦        transmission owner excluded services and connection schedule, and

         ♦        income adjusting events related to the STC.

5.3.     The July report also discussed the need for certain reporting arrangements for:

                  o   capital expenditure (SP Transmission and SHETL)

                  o   payments by NGC to transmission owners, and

                  o   losses

5.4.    This chapter also presents Ofgem’s conclusions on these specific items.


         TO excluded services and connection schedule

         Ofgem’s July report – excluded services

5.5.     Currently, excluded service revenues for SP Transmission and SHETL mainly
        relate to the England-Scotland interconnector and post-Vesting connections.
        Under BETTA, the England-Scotland interconnector will be part of the price
        controls and therefore will not have excluded service related revenues. In
        relation to post-Vesting connections, Ofgem’s July report proposed that under
        BETTA, payments to transmission owners in relation to connections (now from
        NGC rather than users in Scotland) should continue to be treated as excluded
        service revenues by transmission owners. The July report proposed a revised
        excluded services schedule in their licence to apply to transmission owners
        under BETTA, to reflect the incorporation of the England-Scotland interconnector

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 58                       December 2004
        into the price controls and the fact that transmission owners provide services to
        the NGC, and not directly to users.

5.6.    Under BETTA, charges levied by SP Transmission and SHETL on NGC in respect
        of certain connections will be treated by SP Transmission and SHETL as
        excluded services revenues.

5.7.     The July report also proposed that where transmission owners request
        recompense from the GB system operator as a result of changes to outage plans
        made by the GB system operator at short notice, transmission owners will be
        under an obligation to make declarations that the charges accurately reflect costs
        which are reasonable and efficiently incurred. The July report said that if such
        payments are treated as excluded service revenues then excluded services
        schedule will need to include the necessary obligations on transmission owners.

        Respondents’ views – excluded services

5.8.     NGT said that the proposed remuneration regime for changes to the outage
        programme will provide only weak incentives on Scottish TOs to assist in the
        management of constraints because 1) Scottish TOs will not be exposed to the
        cost of constraints and 2) constraints will provide advantageous trading
        conditions for generators including the generation businesses affiliated to
        Scottish TOs. The regime also provides strong incentives for TOs not affiliated
        with the GBSO to maximise cost claims for outage changes. Given the above,
        NGT said it is essential that the prices requested by Scottish TOs for outage
        changes are regulated in a manner which best ensures that they are truly cost-
        reflective and also ensures that outage changes are actually delivered save for
        exceptional circumstances.

         Ofgem’s July report – connection schedule

5.9.     To clarify the basis on which transmission owners should charge NGC as GB
        system operator for connection assets, Ofgem’s July report proposed a
        Connection Schedule (describing the basis on which connections should be
        charged) to the transmission owners licences and presented initial drafting.

        Respondents’ views – connection schedule


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 59                       December 2004
5.10.    SSE said that for post BETTA assets, Ofgem state in paragraph 4.52 that it “does
         not believe that the form of the GB System Operator charging statement should
         constrain the methodology used by transmission owners in charging the GBSO
         for connections”. SSE said this could mean a divergence between the means of
         funding similar assets across the three licensees and would make setting the
         price controls difficult since it would require a different approach for each
         licensee. It could also mean that the GBSO would not have the necessary
         information required to charge its customers. Noting that the methodologies will
         align at the start of BETTA, SSE did not see any merit in TOs developing their
         own charging methodologies.

5.11.    Given that NGC would be exposed to any difference between charges that
         Scottish TO’s may bill the GBSO for post-BETTA connections and the charges
         that the GBSO may recover from users under the connection charging
         methodology, NGT welcomed Ofgem’s clarification that the two charging
         arrangements will align at BETTA go-live39. NGT also welcome the proposal to
         clarify the basis on which Scottish TO’s should charge the GBSO for connection
         assets40.

5.12.    NGT welcomed the proposed relevant objective41, and said that it understands
         that this objective would require consideration to be given to issues that might
         arise if developments to the charging methodology might give rise to
         misalignment between the GBSO charges and TO charges.

         Ofgem’s views

5.13.    Since July, Ofgem has had further discussions with companies regarding
         connections related revenues. For practical reasons, Ofgem sees benefit in
         aligning as far as possible and appropriate, the definition of those connection
         revenues to be treated as excluded services revenues in each of the transmission
         licensees’ licences. However, this should not preclude different treatments of
         expenditures and revenues where this is more suited to accounting



39
   paragraph 4.52, Ofgem’s July report
40
   Appendix 11, Ofgem’s July report
41
   drafted in paragraph 7 part c of Appendix 11, Ofgem’s July report


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 60                       December 2004
        arrangements; for example, where there are particular cost allocation difficulties.
        In the draft licence conditions for SP Transmission and SHETL, Ofgem has
        therefore retained the form of the existing excluded service arrangements in
        relation to connections but with an adjustment to provide for a company specific
        boundary between excluded service revenues and price controlled revenues. In
        order to provide accounting clarity, companies will be required to report to
        Ofgem on the projected and actual revenues received in respect of transmission
        services and separately in respect of connections where these revenues form part
        of the allowed revenues (eg pre-Vesting connections) and also to include this
        information in their regulatory accounts.

5.14.    Ofgem’s July report proposed that transmission owner charges for outage
        changes should be made on a cost reflective basis and this has been included in
        the licence drafting presented at the end of this chapter. These costs may be
        incurred by NGC when it seeks to optimise overall system operations by
        changing previously agreed outage plans. Ofgem recognises that the costs of
        outage rescheduling at short notice are uncertain at this time, and proposes an
        income adjusting provision relating to payments for outages where this can be
        justified, and this is reflected in the licence drafting. Ofgem also recognises that
        the financial incentives for efficiency within these arrangements are weak and
        proposes that the cost impact of any inefficiencies are subject to periodic review
        and, in any case, that incentives for efficiency will be considered as part of the
        main review for 2007, when firm data on the volume of outage changes and
        their costs will be available.

5.15.    Ofgem recognise that there may be occasions where there is a failure to agree
        between the licensee and a transmission owner in relation to:

         ♦        whether a change to the outage plan is a chargeable outage change, or

         ♦        the net costs reasonably incurred by the transmission owner as a result of
                  an outage change.

5.16.    On such occasions, there is a right of dispute to the Authority and an obligation
        on the parties to provide information. To support this provision of information,
        Ofgem is proposing STC changes in relation to the matters which NGC is


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 61                       December 2004
           required to maintain in its register of changes to the outage plan. In essence it is
           proposed that, in addition to the matters already listed, NGC must also maintain
           records as to the net costs incurred by transmission owners as a result of a
           relevant change to the outage plan.

5.17.       Ofgem’s proposals are set down in Appendix 14. This information recorded
           under this proposal may also inform the development of proposals for the 2007
           review.

5.18.       Ofgem has the power42 to direct modifications to the STC during the transition
           period to BETTA go-live, where it considers it necessary or expedient for the
           purposes of implementing BETTA, including to make changes as may be
           required or as may be appropriate for the purposes of reflecting in the STC the
           development of, among other things, the revenue restriction licence conditions
           which are to apply to transmission licensees under BETTA.           It is proposed that,
           subject to consideration of any responses to the Appendix 14 drafting, that
           Ofgem would exercise this power to direct this modification to the STC.

5.19.       NGC raised the issue of the alignment of connection charges between
           transmission owners and NGC with those between NGC and connectees, and
           the potential consequential effect on NGC’s revenue position. The magnitude of
           the effect is uncertain but Ofgem considers that it is likely to be small; Ofgem
           does not therefore propose to address this issue in these price control proposals.
           If this sum should turn out to be more significant, the issue would need to be
           addressed as part of NGC’s roll forward price control review for 2006/7.


            Income adjusting events

5.20.       Ofgem’s July report proposed that an income adjusting event provision should
           be included in the revenue restrictions of all the transmission licensees for
           remunerating costs arising from STC amendments post BETTA go-live (to the
           extent that the costs are material and have not already been allowed for in an
           allowable revenue).



42
     Pursuant to transmission licence standard condition B12, paragraph 7


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 62                             December 2004
           Respondents’ views

5.21.       A respondent supported the use of an income adjusting event mechanism for
           remunerating both costs and savings in the event of STC amendments post
           BETTA, but suggested that there is insufficient public information available for a
           company other than a TO, to raise an IAE in the event of a potential saving
           arising, and that this should be addressed.

5.22.       NGT suggested it would be helpful if it could be confirmed that the omission of
           IAT term is intended to be omitted from NGC’s part 1 revenue restriction.

5.23.       SP Transmission said that it can see a case for introducing such a provision, in
           order to provide a means of recovering unexpected cost increases. However, SP
           Transmission also expressed a concern that incentives may be undermined if
           efficiencies achieved were to be clawed back immediately by means of this
           mechanism.

           Ofgem’s proposals

5.24.       Ofgem notes the broad support for the introduction of an income adjusting event
           (IAE) provision, and considers that introduction of such a provision provides a
           means of encouraging changes to the STC where these drive efficiency across the
           transmission system as a whole. Ofgem believes that the proposed licence
           drafting presented at the end of this chapter provides sufficient flexibility to
           achieve this objective43.

5.25.       Ofgem does not consider it necessary to include the IAE mechanism in more
           than one part of NGC’s revenue restriction since one mechanism will provide
           the appropriate level of remuneration. Ofgem proposes to include it as part of
           NGC’s non-incentivised internal cost control. If there are cost allocation issues
           arising within NGC arising from any IAE decision, these will be addressed at the
           subsequent price control review.


            Reporting arrangements


43
     In this context, Ofgem proposes a threshold of £1m for consideration of an income adjusting event.


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 63                                     December 2004
        Capital expenditure (SP Transmission and SHETL)

5.26.    Chapter 2 sets out Ofgem’s proposals for expenditure reporting. These proposals
        are included the draft licence conditions for SP Transmission and SHETL.

        Payments by NGC to transmission owners

5.27.    In order to provide accounting clarity, Ofgem’s July report proposed that NGC
        should report each year the amounts paid by NGC to each transmission owner
        in the preceding year:

         ♦        under the STC, and

         ♦        under any other contractual arrangement, if any (each such payment to
                  be specified).

5.28.    Ofgem considers that this reporting should form part of the current arrangements
        for financial reporting to Ofgem including the Regulatory Accounts.

        Losses

5.29.    Under BETTA, the continued development of economical and efficient solutions
        will require the licensees to cooperate and exchange information, for example
        on losses. Detailed procedures under the STC are being developed by the
        licensees to support this general requirement. Nevertheless, there may be a
        need to develop incentives in relation to losses, that recognise that the level of
        transmission losses is influenced by both investment decisions and operating
        decisions.

5.30.    Ofgem’s July report proposed that licensees make reports on progress with loss
        reduction measures in order to inform the main review for 2007. SP
        Transmission noted that under BETTA the overall system losses will be
        dependent on the action of both TOs and the GBSO. Development of reporting
        arrangements and incentives could therefore be problematic. Ofgem considers
        that its proposals are consistent with a first stage to addressing this issue.




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 64                        December 2004
5.31.    Ofgem proposes that each licensee compiles and publishes a report each year,
        including information provided by other transmission licensees where
        appropriate.

5.32.    The report by NGC should include

         ♦        an analysis of the losses incurred in the previous formula year on a GB
                  and transmission owner basis, including an assessment of the magnitude
                  and accuracy of the loss figures.

         ♦        projections of losses for the year of the report on a GB and transmission
                  area basis, and

         ♦        the measures it has taken and plans to take as an asset owner to optimise
                  losses in England and Wales.

5.33.    The reports by transmission owners (SP Transmission and SHETL) should focus
        on the measures they have taken and plan to take as asset owners to optimise
        losses in their transmission areas.

5.34.    The proposed reporting arrangements are set out in the draft licence conditions.


         Draft licence modifications

5.35.    The draft licence modifications which are intended to reflect Ofgem’s final
         proposals including the proposals set out above are presented as follows:

         ♦        the draft licence modification for SP Transmission is set out in Appendix
                  15 for the case where BETTA go-live is 1 April 2005, or shortly
                  afterwards

         ♦        the draft licence modification for SHETL is set out in Appendix 16 for the
                  case where BETTA go-live is 1 April 2005, or shortly afterwards, and

         ♦        the draft licence modification for NGC is set out in Appendix 17 for the
                  case where BETTA go-live is 1 April 2005, or shortly afterwards.

5.36.    Although the licence drafting presented here is for the conditions to apply under
         BETTA, it should be noted that the draft conditions applicable to SP

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment

         Transmission and SHETL include drafting reflecting Ofgem’s proposals for the
         roll forward controls since these form an integral part of the controls to apply
         under BETTA.

5.37.    Appended to this document are the latest versions of the proposed drafting
         changes to the revenue restriction licence conditions of transmission licensees.
         Ofgem recognises that this drafting requires further development and will
         progress this work in conjunction with transmission licensees. The drafts are
         change-marked against the versions of the licence conditions published in
         Ofgem’s July report. Changes to the licence conditions implemented after July
         will be included in the development of future drafts.

5.38.    At this stage the draft licence modifications do not include the proposed level of
         allowed revenues set out in this document. These will be included in subsequent
         drafting. It should be noted that the values in the licence modifications will need
         to reflect appropriate price levels and the mechanisms for the pass-through of
         certain items (licence fees and network rates) that are included in the allowed
         revenues.

5.39.    Draft licence modifications related to proposals by Ofgem for the funding of
         transmission investment for renewable generation will be published shortly. This
         licence drafting and the licence drafting presented in this chapter will be
         integrated in due course, as part of the formal process of putting in place licence
         modifications under section 11 of the Electricity Act to apply from 1 April 2005.
         For the present, Ofgem believes these two sets of drafting are sufficiently
         different in their intention for parties to comment on the drafting separately, and
         to comment on any interactions that they perceive.




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 66                       December 2004
 Appendix 1 Related consultations
Ofgem is consulting separately on two areas of work which may lead to adjustments to
the price controls set out in this document:

         ♦        the funding of additional investment in the transmission systems of the
                  three transmission licensees to accommodate renewable generation.
                  Initial proposals were published in August 200444 and further proposals
                  will be published shortly, and

         ♦        Ofgem has recently issued final proposals45 in relation to the electricity
                  transmission network reliability incentive schemes to apply to NGC.
                  Under these proposals NGC will be rewarded or penalised depending on
                  the level of energy unsupplied from the England and Wales grid in any
                  given year compared to a baseline. The report says that there are a
                  number of issues to resolve before incentive arrangements can be
                  introduced for SHETL and SP Transmission, but it is intended to
                  complete this work to coincide with the BETTA go-live date.

Ofgem is also consulting separately on NGC’s SO external cost incentives to apply from
1 April 200546. Initial proposals are to be published shortly. This is a separate part to
NGC’s revenue restrictions and will not lead to adjustments to the final proposals set out
in this document. However, it should be noted that part of these proposals will be a set
of sharing factors to apply to the external SO scheme where costs are higher or lower
than a target, and that the internal SO scheme sharing factors have in the past aligned
with the external SO scheme sharing factors. Changes to the sharing factors can affect
the NGC’s revenues where there is under or over performance against the target.

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 69                       December 2004
 Appendix 3 Impact assessment

Introduction


Section 5A of the Utilities Act 2000 applies where48:


        the Authority is proposing to do anything for the purposes of, or in connection
         with, the carrying out any function exercisable by it, under or by virtue of Part I
         of the Electricity Act 1989; and


        it appears to the Authority that it is important.


Where section 5A of the Utilities Act applies, the Authority must:


        carry out and publish and an Impact Assessment (IA); or


        publish a statement setting out the reasons that it considers that it is unnecessary
         for it to carry out an IA.



Under section 5A, the Authority may, where it considers that it is an urgent case,
determine that it would be impracticable or inappropriate to comply with the
requirement to produce an IA.


In this case, the Authority proposes to implement its conclusions on the roll forward
price controls to apply to SPTL and SHETL and the conclusions on the price controls to
apply to the transmission licensee under BETTA, by exercising its powers under Part 1 of
the Electricity Act 1989, namely by modifying by agreement the licences of the
transmission licensees under section 11 of the Act (as amended).




48
   Section 5A was inserted into the Utilities Act by section 6 the Sustainable Energy Act 2003, with effect
from December 2003.




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 70                                      December 2004
In light of the above, Ofgem considers that it must publish an IA in relation to its
conclusions on the roll forward price controls to apply to SPT and SHETL as set out in
this paper and the price control proposals to apply to the transmission licensees under
BETTA. Ofgem’s IA is set out in this Appendix.


Roll forward price controls (SP Transmission and SHETL)


Objectives


For SHETL and SP Transmission, the current transmission price controls expire on 31
March 2005.


The objectives are to roll forward the price controls for SP Transmission and SHETL
(absent BETTA) to align the price control review dates with those for NGC enabling all
transmission issues (gas and electricity) to be considered together at the next review for
2007. It is proposed to apply these roll forward price controls to SP Transmission and
SHETL if BETTA were delayed.


The roll forward price controls will also form the controls (in relation to SP Transmission
and SHETL) to which the adjustments necessary for BETTA will be made.


Issues


The existing price controls for SP Transmission and SHETL end on 31 March 2005, and
to ensure that the interests of consumers continue to be protected after that date, the “do
nothing” option is not appropriate.


Consideration was given to carrying out a review to set controls for a further 5 year
period. This was rejected as alignment of transmission price control review dates as
early as possible was considered the best solution across gas and electricity, leading to a
                                                                                                     49
decision to roll forward the price controls for the two year period (2005/6 and 2006/7) .




49
  Review of transmission price controls from 2005: SP Transmission Ltd, Scottish Hydro-Electric
Transmission Ltd, Initial thoughts, March 2004, Ofgem 52/04


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 71                                  December 2004
Consideration was then given as to the approach to be taken in developing these roll
forward controls50. One option was to carry out a full review for a 2 year period to
allow alignment of price control reviews in 2007. This option was not considered a
proportionate approach given that it is proposed that the controls would only apply for 2
years. Therefore a more limited review has been carried out.

The main effects of the roll forward price control proposals are on SP Transmission and
SHETL. This is discussed in more detail in Chapter 2. The level of the allowed revenues
does affect transmission users through the level of transmission charges.




The key issues as set out in this paper are:

       a) Opex and capex allowances
           These have been set to provide a level of revenues to the transmission licensees
           which reflect licensees’ projections of transmission requirements in 2005/6 and
           2006/7 (details of allowance are given in Chapter 2).


       b) Rolling opex and capex incentives
           When considering rolling capital and operating expenditure incentives, the base
           period covering April 2000 to March 2005, needs to be considered separately
           from the roll forward period covering April 2005 to March 2007.


           It is proposed to apply rolling expenditure incentives in the base period from the
           year 2003/4 using a methodology similar to that developed as part of the
           distribution price control review final proposals (DPCR4). It is not proposed to
           implement rolling opex or capex incentives for SP Transmission and SHETL for
           the roll forward period. This is because the capex and opex allowances have not
           been subject to the level of scrutiny that would be appropriate to a full review,
           and the cost allocation definitions necessary to make any incentive scheme
           robust have not investigated. For more details, see Chapter 2.




50
     Transmission price controls and BETTA, Update, May 2004, Ofgem 107/04


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 72                        December 2004
    c) Pass-though of network rates
         It is proposed to allow SP Transmission and SHETL to recover the actual costs
         incurred on network rates. The impact of the pass-through of network rates on
         users will depend on the outcome of the tax settlement.


    d) Cost of capital and treatment of tax
         The cost of capital for the SP Transmission and SHETL reviews is aligned with
         the proposals under DPCR4. Ofgem has not scrutinised the companies’ tax
         calculations to the extent carried out as part of DPCR4 and there is the risk that
         moving directly to a post tax cost of capital could unduly disadvantage
         consumers or licensees. It is therefore proposed that cost of capital should be set
         on a pre-tax basis, consistent with the DPCR4 assumptions, and augmented to
         allow for the level of tax allowances calculated as part of DPCR4. This approach
         is not unreasonable since both SP Transmission and SHETL have affiliated
         distribution companies. These affiliated companies are under the same
         ownership and management as and therefore likely to have similar approaches
         to accounting and tax policies. For more details, see Chapter 2.


Risks and unintended consequences


        The main risk in any price control is that the regulator sets the maximum
         allowed revenue either (materially) too high or too low because of information
         asymmetry between the regulator and the company. This risk is likely to be
         larger in a roll forward control than a full review.
         Ofgem has mitigated this risk by introducing an expenditure reporting regime on
         SP Transmission and SHETL (supported by licence obligations), particularly
         focused on capital expenditure, to identify project costs, the benefits provided by
         each project, and explanations for changes.

        An important issue is whether the price controls provide appropriate incentives
         to companies to deliver the outputs required of them and to continue seeking
         out efficiency savings. In this context, it is not proposed to implement rolling
         opex or capex incentives for SP Transmission and SHETL for the roll forward
         period. The formulation of rolling expenditure incentives will therefore be part
         of the work for the 2007 review rather than as part of the roll forward review. It

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 73                       December 2004
         is recognised that, as a result, there is a risk that the incentives to drive for
         efficiency may not be as strong in the roll forward period compared to a longer
         duration price control (eg 5 years).

Impacts on competition


It is anticipated that there will be no impact on competition as compared with the
existing price controls.


Costs and benefits


The level of scrutiny carried out by Ofgem in the roll forward period has been less than
would be appropriate in a full review. Consequently, the resources employed by Ofgem
and the companies have also been less, striking a balance between the extent of the
work programme to develop price control proposals and the protection of the interests
of consumers.


Overall, the impacts on licensees are:


        for SP Transmission, the 2005/6 revenues increase by 9% compared to 2004/5,
         and


        for SHETL, the 2005/6 revenues increase by 4% compared to 2004/5.


    For more details, including the proposed level of allowed revenues for each
    licensee, see Chapter 2.


Environmental impacts


        It is anticipated that there will be no difference in the impact on the environment
         as compared with the existing price controls. Overall therefore, it is anticipated
         that the environmental impact of the proposals will be neutral.

Security of supply considerations




Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 74                          December 2004
        It is anticipated that there will be no impact on security of supply as compared
         with the existing price controls. No changes are proposed to the form of the
         existing controls in this regard. Expenditure allowances for companies are
         higher than levels in existing controls providing for the development and
         maintenance of transmission assets to support the maintenance of security of
         supply standards.

Distributional effects


        No changes are proposed to the form of the existing controls in this regard. It is
         therefore anticipated that there will be no significant distributional effects as
         compared with the existing price controls.


Securing compliance


        The new price controls will need to be implemented through licence
         modifications.


        There will be a statutory consultation commencing in February 2005. If
         companies do not accept the licence modifications, Ofgem expects to refer the
         matter to the Competition Commission for a decision.


        Once the new price controls have been implemented Ofgem will monitor
         companies’ compliance against the relevant licence conditions in the normal
         way.




BETTA price controls (SPTL, SHETL and NGC)



Objectives




        to develop proposals to reflect the new trading and transmission arrangements
         that will apply under BETTA. The proposals include the costs of implementing


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 75                        December 2004
         BETTA incurred by transmission licensees and inclusion of the England-Scotland
         interconnector into the price controls. These proposals are developed as
         adjustments to the roll forward controls for SP Transmission and SHETL, and to
         NGC’s existing price control.



Issues


Given that BETTA has been given effect through the Energy Act 2004 and that the
Secretary of State has exercised her powers under that Act to implement BETTA,
Ofgem’s view is that no other options were considered appropriate.


In relation to the price controls to apply to transmission licensees under BETTA, the key
issues (discussed in more detail in chapters 3 and 4) are:


         a) BETTA implementation costs
             To deliver BETTA, the licensees will need to put in place new procedures,
             systems and arrangements that provide the necessary interfaces between
             each other. The associated development costs are additional to those
             expected at the time of the last price control reviews. For further detail see
             chapter 4.


         b) Enduring operating cost changes
             The current price controls and incentives reflect the current sets of activities
             undertaken by licensees. The split of functions between transmission owners
             and the GB system operator under BETTA will change the activities, and
             adjustments to price controls need to be made to reflect licensees’ costs in
             performing these changed activities. In particular, NGC will incur additional
             costs to perform its new role as GB system operator.
             NGC will incur additional costs with transmission owners for the
             rescheduling of outages at short notice. The volume and cost of these outage
             changes is uncertain. The lower estimate of cost is included in the price
             controls complemented by an income adjusting event provision, should the
             costs turnout to be significantly different from those projected.
             In addition, the price controls of all transmission licensees will need to
             remunerate the operating costs related to the England-Scotland

Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 76                         December 2004
             interconnector assets that are currently outside the price controls.
             For further details see chapters 4 and 5.


         c) Valuation of the England-Scotland interconnector on its inclusion into the
             price controls
             In making proposals for the financial arrangements to take effect when the
             exisiting contractual arrangements for the England-Scotland Interconnector
             cease to have effect, Ofgem will need to satisfy itself that the proposals are
             consistent with its statutory duties and that, among other things, the
             proposed arrangements are reasonable in all the circumstances. This will
             include identifying the extent to which each licensee will be affected, the
             interests they will retain under BETTA and, in its proposals, balancing these
             against the public interest. The methodologies considered for valuing the
             interconnector are set out in chapter 351, which also presents the proposed
             valuation.


         d) Introduction of ‘connection PLUGS’ to Scotland.
             The introduction of BETTA will require connectees in Scotland to enter into
             new connection agreements with NGC. This combined with the introduction
             of the PLUGS methodology means that SP Transmission and SHETL will
             need to repay significant capital amounts (greater than £10m) to connectees
             in respect of previous capital contributions made by the connectees. Also in
             the roll forward period, SP Transmission and SHETL will need to make
             additional capital expenditures on infrastructure that would, prior to BETTA,
             have been funded by connectees. For further details see chapter 4.


Risks and unintended consequences


            The main risk in any price control is that the regulator sets the maximum
             allowed revenue either (materially) too high or too low. To minimise the
             risks of this, for BETTA implementation costs and capital repayments,



51
  For further information on methodologies, see: Transmission price controls and BETTA, Draft Proposals,
July 2004, Ofgem 182/04


Transmission price controls and BETTA: Final proposals and impact assessment
Office of Gas and Electricity Markets                 77                                  December 2004
             licensees will be required to provide audited reports on the actual
             expenditure incurred. Adjustments to the price controls will be made for
             differences between projected costs and appropriately incurred actual costs.
             For further details see chapters 4 and 5.

            The issues surrounding the valuation of the England-Scotland interconnector
             are discussed in Chapter 3.

            There is the risk that the STC is not perfectly formulated to provide for
             efficient interactions between transmission licensees. To mitigate this,
             Ofgem proposes an income adjusting event provision to encourage licensees
             to propose changes to the STC that drive efficiency. (see Chapter 5)

Impacts on competition


            The impact of BETTA generally on competition is discussed in the RIA for the
             Energy Act52 published by the Secretary of State. Ofgem considers that the
             price controls in themselves do not directly affect competition. The
             consultation on the proposed transmission charging methodologies of the
             GB system operator is also relevant53 in relation to the impact on
             customers54.

Costs and benefits


The main effects are on transmission licensees (SP Transmission, SHETL and NGC). See
Chapter 4 for more details. The level of the allowed revenues does affect transmission
users through the level of transmission charges.



The following table shows the increase in overall transmission sector costs to users
resulting from the BETTA price control adjustments.

    British Electricity Trading And Transmission Arrangements, Regulatory Impact Assessment:

   Further analysis will be carried out as part of the approval process for NGC’s charging methodology.
